All devas, humans, and asuras in the world consider that the present Śākyamuni Buddha, having left the Śākya palace, went not far from the city of Gayā, sat at the place of awakening, and attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

However, Good children! It has been immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of kalpas since I truly attained Buddhahood.

Suppose there were five hundred million billion inconceivable incalculable great three-thousand-fold worlds; if someone were to pulverize them into dust motes, and passing eastward across five hundred million billion inconceivable incalculable lands, dropped a single dust mote; continuing eastward in this manner until all these dust motes were exhausted. Good children! What do you think: could these worlds be conceived, calculated, and their numbers known?”

Maitreya Bodhisattva and the others addressed the Buddha together: “World-Honored One! These worlds are immeasurable, boundless, beyond the reach of calculation, nor can mental power reach them. All Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas, with their uncontaminated wisdom, cannot conceive or know their limits. We, dwelling on the ground of not-returning, also cannot penetrate this matter. World-Honored One! Such worlds as these are immeasurable and boundless.”

Then the Buddha addressed the assembly of great Bodhisattvas: “Good children! I will now clearly proclaim to you: if all these worlds, both where dust motes were dropped and where none were dropped, were entirely pulverized into dust motes, each dust mote counted as a kalpa, the time since I attained Buddhahood exceeds even this by hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of incalculable kalpas.

From that time onward, I have constantly been in this Sahā world expounding the Dharma, teaching and transforming; and in other places, in hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of incalculable lands, guiding and benefiting wandering beings.

Good children! During this interval, I spoke of Dīpaṃkara Buddha and others, and also spoke of them entering nirvāṇa; all these were distinguished through expedient means.

Good children! If there are wandering beings who come to where I am, I, with the Buddha eye, observe whether their faculties of faith and the like are sharp or dull, and according to what is appropriate to liberate them, in various places I speak of myself under different names and differing ages, and also manifest saying that I will enter nirvāṇa; through various expedient means, I expound the subtle, wondrous Dharma, able to cause wandering beings to generate joyful minds.

Good children! The Tathāgata, seeing wandering beings who delight in small dharmas, of meager virtue and heavy defilements, speaks to these persons: ‘In my youth I left home and attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi.’ Yet in truth, since I attained Buddhahood it has been long and distant like this; it is merely through expedient means to teach and transform wandering beings, causing them to enter the Buddha path, that I speak thus.

Good children! The scriptures expounded by the Tathāgata are all for the sake of liberating wandering beings: whether speaking of his own body or speaking of other bodies; whether displaying his own body or displaying other bodies; whether displaying his own deeds or displaying other deeds—all that is spoken is genuine and without falsehood.

For what reason? The Tathāgata knows and sees the marks of the three realms as they truly are: without birth and death, without receding or emerging; neither dwelling in the world nor entering extinction; non-real, non-empty, non-such, non-different; not viewing the three realms as the three realms view them. Matters such as these, the Tathāgata sees clearly without error.

Because wandering beings have various natures, various desires, various actions, and various recollections and discriminations, wishing to cause them to give rise to wholesome roots, through various causal conditions, parables, and words, he expounds the Dharma in various ways; the Buddha activities performed have never ceased for an instant.

Thus, since I attained Buddhahood, it has been exceedingly long and distant; my lifespan is immeasurable, incalculable kalpas, constantly abiding and never extinguished.

Good children! The lifespan I accomplished by originally practicing the Bodhisattva path is even now not yet exhausted, and exceeds the previous number twofold. Yet now, not being genuine extinction, I proclaim: ‘I will take extinction.’ Through this expedient means, the Tathāgata teaches and transforms wandering beings.

For what reason? If the Buddha were to abide long in the world, persons of meager virtue would not plant wholesome roots; poor and base, greedily attached to the five desires, they would enter the net of recollections and false views. If they saw that the Tathāgata was constantly present and not extinguished, they would give rise to arrogance and become negligent, unable to generate the thought that he is difficult to encounter, or a mind of reverence.

Therefore, the Tathāgata expediently speaks: ‘Bhikṣus, you should know! The appearance of Buddhas in the world is difficult to encounter.’

For what reason? Persons of meager virtue, passing through immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of kalpas, some behold a Buddha, and some do not; on account of this matter, I speak these words: ‘Bhikṣus! The Tathāgata is difficult to behold.’ These wandering beings, hearing such words, will certainly give rise to the thought that he is difficult to encounter, harboring longing in their hearts, thirsting for the Buddha, and will then plant wholesome roots. Therefore, although the Tathāgata does not truly pass into extinction, he speaks of extinction.

Furthermore, Good children! The Dharma of all Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, is like this; for the sake of liberating wandering beings, all is genuine and without falsehood.

Suppose a skilled physician, intelligent and clear-sighted, versed in formulas and medicines, skillfully treats all diseases. The man has many children—ten, twenty, or up to a hundred. Due to some affair, he travels far to another country.

Afterward, the children drink poisonous medicine from elsewhere; the medicine takes effect, causing delirium and agony, and they roll writhing upon the ground.

At that time, their father returns home. Among the children who drank the poison, some have lost their original senses, while others have not. Seeing their father from afar, they all rejoice greatly, kneeling and inquiring: ‘Peacefully and safely have you returned! We, foolish and deluded, mistakenly took poisonous medicine; pray rescue and treat us, and grant us life again!’

The father, seeing his children afflicted with such suffering, relies on formulas and seeks fine medicinal herbs, fully endowed with color, scent, and fine flavor, poundings, siftings, and mixing them together, giving them to his children to take, saying: ‘This great, excellent medicine is fully endowed with color, scent, and fine flavor; you may take it; it will quickly eliminate suffering and afflictions, and you will have no further tribulations.’

Those among the children who have not lost their senses, seeing this excellent medicine with both color and scent being fine, immediately take it, and their illness is completely cured.

The others who have lost their senses, seeing their father arrive, although also joyfully inquiring and seeking treatment for their illness, when given the medicine, refuse to take it.

For what reason? Because the poison has penetrated deeply, causing them to lose their original senses; regarding this medicine of fine color and scent, they consider it not delicious.

The father has this thought: ‘These children are to be pitied; struck by poison, their minds are entirely inverted. Although seeing me with joy and seeking treatment, they refuse to take such fine medicine. I must now devise an expedient means to cause them to take this medicine.’

Immediately he says: ‘You should know! I am now old and decrepit, and the time of death has arrived. This fine medicine I now leave here; you may take it; do not worry that you will not recover.’

Having given this instruction, he travels again to another country, and dispatches a messenger to return and announce: ‘Your father has already died.’

At that time, the children, hearing that their father has passed away, are grieved and afflicted in heart, thinking: ‘If our father were alive, he would pity us and rescue us; now he has abandoned us and died far away in another country.’ Reflecting that they are orphaned and destitute, having no further protector, constantly harboring sorrow, their minds finally awaken; they then realize that this medicine has fine color, scent, and flavor, immediately take it, and the illness of poison is completely cured. The father, hearing that his children have all recovered, soon returns, causing them all to behold him.

Good children! What do you think: could anyone say that this skilled physician is guilty of falsehood?”

“No, World-Honored One!”

The Buddha said: “I too am like this. Since I attained Buddhahood, it has been immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of inconceivable numbers of incalculable kalpas; for the sake of wandering beings, through the power of expedient means, I say that I will take extinction; yet none can say according to Dharma that I am guilty of falsehood.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Since I attained Buddhahood,

The kalpas that have elapsed,

Are immeasurable hundreds of thousands,

Tens of billions of ages incalculable;

Constantly expounding the Dharma, teaching and transforming

Countless billions of wandering beings,

Causing them to enter the Buddha path.

Since then, throughout immeasurable kalpas,

For the sake of liberating wandering beings,

Expediently I manifest nirvāṇa;

Yet in truth I do not pass into extinction,

Constantly dwelling here to expound the Dharma.

I constantly abide here,

Using all spiritual powers,

So that perverted wandering beings,

Though near, do not see me.

Multitudes seeing me enter extinction,

Widely make offerings to my relics,

All harboring longing in their hearts,

Giving rise to thirsty yearning.

When wandering beings have become faithful and subdued,

Upright and gentle in mind,

Single-mindedly wishing to behold the Buddha,

Not begrudging physical life;

Then I, together with the Saṅgha,

Appear on Vulture Peak.

I then tell wandering beings:

“I am constantly here without extinction;

Through the power of expedient means,

I manifest having extinction and non-extinction.”

In other lands where there are wandering beings,

Reverent, faithful, and joyful,

I also in their midst,

Expound the unexcelled Dharma.

You, not hearing this,

Merely consider that I have passed into extinction.

I see wandering beings,

Submerged in suffering and afflictions;

Therefore I do not manifest my body,

Causing them to give rise to thirsty yearning;

Because of their hearts’ longing,

I then appear to expound the Dharma for them.

Spiritual powers are like this,

Throughout incalculable kalpas,

Constantly on Vulture Peak,

And in various other dwellings.

When wandering beings see the kalpa end,

Burned by a great fire,

This land of mine is safe and peaceful,

Devas and humans constantly filling it.

Gardens, groves, halls, and pavilions,

Adorned with various treasures,

Jewel trees with abundant flowers and fruits,

Where wandering beings play and rejoice.

Devas strike heavenly drums,

Constantly making various music,

Raining down māndārava flowers,

Scattering them over the Buddha and the great assembly.

My pure land is undestroyed,

Yet the multitudes see it burned away,

Grief, terror, and all suffering,

Completely filling it like this.

These sinful wandering beings,

Through causal conditions of evil karma,

Pass through incalculable kalpas,

Without hearing the names of the Three Jewels.

Those who cultivate merits,

Gentle, upright, and direct,

All behold my body,

Dwelling here and expounding the Dharma.

At times for this assembly,

Speaking of the Buddha’s immeasurable lifespan;

For those who only after long behold the Buddha,

Speaking of the Buddha being difficult to meet.

My power of wisdom is like this,

The light of wisdom illuminating boundlessly,

Lifespan of countless kalpas,

Attained through long cultivation of karma.

You who possess wisdom,

Do not give rise to doubt in this!

Sever it and cause it to end forever;

The Buddha’s words are real and not false.

Like the physician skillfully using expedient means,

To cure his deranged children,

Being truly alive, yet saying he has died;

None can say he spoke falsehood.

I too am the father of the world,

Rescuing all from suffering and affliction;

Because common mortals are perverted,

Being truly present, I say I pass into extinction.

Because constantly seeing me,

They give rise to arrogant minds,

Negligent, attached to the five desires,

Falling into evil destinies.

I constantly know wandering beings,

Whether practicing the path or not practicing the path;

According to what is appropriate to liberate them,

I expound various dharmas for them.

Constantly having this thought within myself:

“By what means can I cause wandering beings,

To enter unexcelled wisdom,

And swiftly accomplish the Buddha-body?”

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XVII

Distinção dos méritos

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, when the great assembly heard the Buddha speak of his lifespan and kalpas being long and distant like this, immeasurable, boundless, incalculable wandering beings attained great benefit.

Then the World-Honored One addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “Ajita! When I spoke of this Tathāgata’s lifespan being long and distant, wandering beings numbering six hundred and eighty thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands attained the forbearance of non-arising dharmas;

Furthermore, a thousand times more Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas attained the dhāraṇī door of hearing and upholding;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a single world attained joyful and unhindered eloquence;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a single world attained hundreds of thousands of millions of immeasurable rotating dhāraṇīs;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a great three-thousand-fold worlds became able to turn the irreversible wheel of Dharma;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of two thousand middle universes became able to turn the pure wheel of Dharma;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a small universe will attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi after eight rebirths;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of four four-continents will attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi after four rebirths;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of three four-continents will attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi after three rebirths;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of two four-continents will attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi after two rebirths;

Furthermore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of one four-continent will attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi after a single rebirth;

Furthermore, wandering beings equal to the dust motes of eight worlds all generated the mind of anuttarasamyaksambodhi.”

When the Buddha spoke of these Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas attaining great Dharma benefits, from open space rained down māndārava flowers and mahā-māndārava flowers, scattering over the Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of jewel trees, and scattering over Śākyamuni Buddha and Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata, who had long passed into extinction, seated upon lion seats within the seven-jeweled stūpa, and also scattering over all the great Bodhisattvas and the fourfold assembly.

There also rained fine powdered sandalwood and aloeswood incense. In open space, heavenly drums naturally sounded, their wondrous tones deep and far-reaching. There also rained a thousand kinds of heavenly garments, draped with necklaces of pearls, mani jewels, and wish-fulfilling jewels, pervading the nine directions. Censers of various jewels burned priceless incense, naturally pervading all around, making offerings to the great assembly.

Above each Buddha stood Bodhisattvas holding banners and canopies, ascending successively up to the Brahmā Heaven. These Bodhisattvas, with wondrous voices, sang immeasurable hymns praising the Buddhas.

Then Maitreya Bodhisattva arose from his seat, bared his right shoulder, joined his palms toward the Buddha, and spoke gāthās:

The Buddha speaks a rare Dharma,

Never heard in the past;

The World-Honored One has great power,

His lifespan beyond measure.

Countless Buddha-children,

Hearing the World-Honored One distinguish

And speak of attaining Dharma benefits,

Have joy pervading their bodies.

Some dwell on the ground of non-retrogression,

Some attain dhāraṇīs,

Some unhindered joyful eloquence,

Tens of billions of rotating total retentions.

Or Bodhisattvas equal to the dust motes

Of a great three-thousand-fold worlds,

Each able to turn

The irreversible wheel of Dharma.

Furthermore, Bodhisattvas equal to the dust motes

Of a middle universe,

Each able to turn

The pure wheel of Dharma.

Furthermore, Bodhisattvas equal to the dust motes

Of a small universe,

With eight rebirths remaining,

Will accomplish the Buddha path.

Furthermore, four, three, or two,

Such four-continents,

Bodhisattvas equal to their dust motes,

Will become Buddhas according to their number of births.

Or Bodhisattvas equal to the dust motes

Of a single four-continent,

With a single rebirth remaining,

Will accomplish all-knowledge.

Wandering beings such as these,

Hearing of the Buddha’s long lifespan,

Attain immeasurable, uncontaminated,

Pure fruits and retributions.

Furthermore, wandering beings equal to the dust motes

Of eight worlds,

Hearing the Buddha speak of his lifespan,

All generate the unexcelled resolve.

The World-Honored One expounds immeasurable,

Inconceivable dharmas,

Bringing abundant benefit,

Boundless like empty space.

Raining heavenly māndārava

And mahā-māndārava flowers;

Śakras and Brahmās like Ganges sands,

Arrive from countless Buddha lands.

Raining sandalwood and aloeswood,

Falling profusely in confusion,

Like birds flying down from the sky,

Scattered as offerings to the Buddhas.

Heavenly drums in open space,

Naturally emit wondrous sounds;

Heavenly garments of thousands of kinds,

Whirling down as they descend.

Exquisite censers of various jewels,

Burning priceless incense,

Naturally pervading all around,

Making offerings to all the World-Honored Ones.

That great assembly of Bodhisattvas,

Holding seven-jeweled banners and canopies,

Lofty and wondrous in tens of billions of kinds,

Successively reaching the Brahmā Heaven.

Before each of the Buddhas,

Precious pillars suspend victory banners,

And with tens of millions of verses,

They sing praises to the Tathāgatas.

Various matters such as these,

Never seen in the past;

Hearing of the Buddha’s immeasurable lifespan,

All rejoice.

The Buddha’s fame pervades the ten directions,

Widely benefiting wandering beings,

All endowed with wholesome roots,

To aid the unexcelled resolve.

Then the Buddha addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “Ajita! If there are wandering beings who, hearing of the Buddha’s lifespan being long and distant like this, can give rise to even a single thought of faith and understanding, the virtues and merits they attain have no limit.

If there is a good man or good woman who, for the sake of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, throughout eighty billion inconceivable kalpas practices the five pāramitās—dāna-pāramitā, śīla-pāramitā, kṣānti-pāramitā, vīrya-pāramitā, and dhyāna-pāramitā—excluding prajñā-pāramitā: compared to the former merit, it does not equal one part in a hundred, one part in a thousand, or one part in hundreds of thousands of millions of billions; even calculation and parables cannot know it. If a good man or good woman possesses such merit, that they should regress from anuttarasamyaksambodhi is impossible.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If a person seeks the Buddha’s wisdom,

Throughout eighty billion

inconceivable kalpas,

Practicing the five pāramitās;

During all these kalpas,

Giving offerings to the Buddhas,

Pratyekabuddha disciples,

And assemblies of Bodhisattvas;

Rare food and drink,

Superior clothing and bedding,

Erecting vihāras of sandalwood,

Adorned with gardens and groves;

Givings such as these,

All subtle and wondrous,

Throughout all these kalpas,

Dedicating them to the Buddha path;

Or upholding the prohibitive precepts,

Pure and without flaw,

Seeking the unexcelled path,

Praised by all the Buddhas;

Or practicing patience,

Dwelling on the ground of gentle compliance,

Even if various evils are inflicted,

His mind is unswayed;

Those who have attained the Dharma,

Harboring arrogant pride,

By these being slighted and distressed,

He is able to endure this as well;

Or diligently vigorous,

His resolve and mindfulness constantly firm,

Throughout immeasurable billions of kalpas,

Single-minded without resting;

And throughout countless kalpas,

Dwelling in leisure places,

Whether sitting or walking,

Dispelling sleep and collecting his mind;

By these causal conditions,

Able to give rise to dhyāna meditations,

For eighty billion kalpas,

Peacefully abiding with mind undistracted;

Upholding the merit of this single-mindedness,

Vowing to seek the unexcelled path:

“May I attain all-knowledge,

Exhausting the limits of dhyāna meditation.”

This person, throughout hundreds of thousands

Of billions of kalpas,

Practices these various merits,

As spoken above;

Yet if there are good men and women,

Who hear me speak of my lifespan,

And give rise to even a single thought of faith,

Their merit surpasses the former!

If a person is completely free

Of all doubts and regrets,

Believing with deep mind for an instant,

Their merit is like this.

Those Bodhisattvas

Who have practiced the path for immeasurable kalpas,

Hearing me speak of my lifespan,

Are able to believe and receive it.

Such persons as these,

Receive this scripture upon their crowns:

“May I in future ages,

Have a long lifespan to liberate wandering beings;

Just as today’s World-Honored One,

King among the Śākyas,

Roars the lion’s roar at the place of awakening,

Expounding the Dharma without fear.

May we in future worlds,

Revered by all,

When seated at the place of awakening,

Speak of our lifespans likewise!”

If there are those of deep mind,

Pure, upright, and direct,

Learned, able to retain all,

Understanding the Buddha’s words according to their meaning,

Such persons as these,

Have no doubts in this matter.

“Furthermore, Ajita! If someone hears of the Buddha’s lifespan being long and distant, and understands the intent of these words, the merits attained by this person have no limit, and can give rise to the unexcelled wisdom of the Tathāgata.

How much more so if one widely hears this scripture or teaches others to hear it; if one upholds it oneself or teaches others to uphold it; if one copies it oneself or teaches others to copy it; or if with flowers, incense, necklaces, banners, canopies, scented oil, and ghee lamps, one makes offerings to the scriptural scrolls—the virtues and merits of this person are immeasurable and boundless, able to generate all-knowledge!

Ajita! If a good man or good woman, hearing me speak of my lifespan being long and distant, believes and understands with a deep mind, they will see the Buddha constantly dwelling on Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa, surrounded by great Bodhisattvas and Śrāvakas, expounding the Dharma.

They will also see this Sahā world: its ground of lapis lazuli, flat and level, bordered with Jāmbūnada gold along eight roads, with rows of jewel trees; towers and pavilions all made of jewels, with the Bodhisattva assemblies dwelling within them. If someone can contemplate in this manner, you should know that this is the mark of deep faith and understanding.

Furthermore, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, if someone hears this scripture and does not slander it, but gives rise to a mind of rejoicing in accord, you should know that this is already the mark of deep faith and understanding; how much more so those who read, recite, receive, and uphold it—such a person bears the Tathāgata upon their crown!

Ajita! This good man or good woman does not need to erect stūpas and temples for me, nor build monasteries and make offerings of the four requisites to the Saṅgha.

For what reason? This good man or good woman, receiving, upholding, reading, and reciting this scripture, has already erected stūpas, built monasteries, and made offerings to the Saṅgha. They have already erected seven-jeweled stūpas for the Buddha’s relics, lofty and broad, tapering gradually up to the Brahmā Heaven, hanging banners, canopies, and jewel bells; offering flowers, incense, necklaces, powdered incense, paste incense, burning incense, drums, music, flutes, pipes, harps, various dances, singing hymns with wondrous voices—they have already made such offerings throughout immeasurable thousands of billions of kalpas.

Ajita! If after my extinction, hearing this scripture, someone can receive and uphold it, whether copying it oneself or teaching others to copy it, they have already erected monasteries, making thirty-two halls of red sandalwood, eight tāla trees in height, lofty, broad, and adorned, where hundreds of thousands of bhikṣus dwell, complete with gardens, bathing ponds, walkways, meditation caves, clothing, food, drink, bedding, medicine, and all articles of joy filling them. Such monasteries, halls, and pavilions, hundreds of thousands of millions, immeasurable in number, have already been offered directly before me and the Bhikṣu-Saṅgha.

Therefore I say: ‘After the Tathāgata’s extinction, if someone receives, upholds, reads, recites, expounds it for others, copies it oneself, or teaches others to copy it, making offerings to the scriptural scrolls, they do not need to erect stūpas and temples, nor build monasteries and make offerings to the Saṅgha.’

How much more so if someone can uphold this scripture while concurrently practicing giving, morality, patience, vigor, single-mindedness, and wisdom—their virtue is supreme, immeasurable, and boundless!

Just as empty space in the east, west, south, north, four intermediate directions, zenith, and nadir is immeasurable and boundless; this person’s virtues and merits are also like this, immeasurable and boundless, swiftly arriving at all-knowledge.

If a person reads, recites, receives, and upholds this scripture, expounds it for others, copies it oneself or teaches others to copy it; and furthermore can erect stūpas, build monasteries, make offerings and praises to the Śrāvaka-Saṅgha, praise the Bodhisattvas’ merits with hundreds of thousands of millions of methods of praise, and for others’ sake explain this Lotus Sūtra through various causal conditions according to its meaning; and furthermore can purely uphold the precepts, dwell together with the gentle, be patient and free of anger, firm in resolve and mindfulness, constantly prize seated meditation, attaining deep samādhis, vigorous and heroic, embracing all wholesome dharmas, of sharp faculties and wisdom, skillfully answering difficult questions:

Ajita! If after my extinction, good men and good women who receive, uphold, read, and recite this scripture possess such wholesome virtues as these, you should know that these persons have already set out for the place of awakening, drawing near to anuttarasamyaksambodhi, seated beneath the tree of the path.

Ajita! Wherever these good men and good women sit, stand, or walk, in that place one should immediately erect a stūpa; all devas and humans should make offerings to it as they would to a stūpa of the Buddha.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If after my extinction,

One can uphold this scripture,

This person’s merit is boundless,

As spoken above;

This is fully endowed with

All offerings.

Erecting a stūpa for relics,

Adorned with the seven treasures,

Its spires exceedingly high and broad,

Tapering up to the Brahmā Heaven,

Jewel bells in tens of billions,

Stirred by wind, emitting wondrous sounds.

And throughout immeasurable kalpas,

Making offerings to this stūpa:

Flowers, incense, necklaces,

Heavenly garments, and music,

Burning scented oil and ghee lamps,

Constantly illuminating all around.

In the evil world of the Final Dharma age,

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Have already, as spoken above,

Perfected all offerings.

If one can uphold this scripture,

It is as if the Buddha were present,

Using ox-head sandalwood,

To build monasteries as offerings.

Thirty-two halls,

Eight tāla trees high,

Superior delicacies and wondrous clothing,

Bedding fully provided,

Dwellings for hundreds of thousands,

Gardens and bathing ponds,

Walkways and meditation caves,

All adorned and fine.

If someone with a faithful mind,

Receives, upholds, reads, recites, and copies,

Or teaches others to copy,

And makes offerings to the scrolls,

Scattering flowers, incense, powdered incense,

With sumanā, campaka,

And atimuktaka,

Scented oils constantly burned:

Those who make offerings like this,

Attain immeasurable merit;

Just as empty space is boundless,

Their merit is also like this.

How much more so upholding this scripture,

Concurrently with giving and morality,

Patience, delighting in meditation,

Free of anger and evil speech,

Reverent toward stūpas and shrines,

Humble before bhikṣus,

Far removed from arrogance,

Constantly contemplating wisdom,

Not angry when questioned with difficulties,

Explaining in accord with the Dharma;

If one can practice such conduct,

Their merits are beyond measure.

If one sees this Dharma teacher,

Accomplished in such virtues,

One should scatter heavenly flowers,

Cover his body with heavenly robes,

Bowing with heads touching his feet,

Generating the thought of him as the Buddha.

And one should have this thought:

“Before long he will go to the tree of the path,

Attaining the uncontaminated and unconditioned,

Widely benefiting devas and humans.”

In the place where he dwells,

Walking, sitting, or lying down,

Or even speaking a single verse,

In that place one should erect a stūpa,

Adorning it wonderfully,

Making various offerings.

When a Buddha-child dwells upon this ground,

It is enjoyed by the Buddha;

He is constantly within it,

Walking, sitting, and lying down.

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XVIII

Os méritos da aceitação jubilosa

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! If there is a good man or good woman who, hearing this Lotus Sūtra, rejoices in accord, how much merit does that person attain?”

And he spoke gāthās:

After the World-Honored One passes into extinction,

If there are those who hear this scripture,

If they can rejoice in accord,

How much merit do they attain?

At that time, the Buddha addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “Ajita! After the Tathāgata’s extinction, if a bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, upāsikā, or other wise person, whether aged or young, hearing this scripture, rejoices in accord, emerges from the Dharma assembly, and goes elsewhere—whether in a monastery, an empty leisure place, a city, an alley, a village, or a hamlet—and according to what they heard, expounds it according to their capacity for parents, relatives, good friends, and acquaintances; and these people, having heard it, rejoice in accord and in turn teach others; and those other people, having heard it, also rejoice in accord and in turn teach others; passing along in this manner successively to the fiftieth person:

Ajita! The merit of joyful acceptance of that fiftieth good man or good woman, I will now speak of it; listen well!

Suppose in four hundred million billion incalculable worlds, all wandering beings of the six destinies and four modes of birth—egg-born, womb-born, moisture-born, and transformation-born, whether having form or formless, conscious or unconscious, neither conscious nor unconscious, footless, two-footed, four-footed, or many-footed, all such as are numbered among wandering beings—suppose someone seeking merit gives them all the articles of entertainment according to their desires. To each wandering being, he gives Jambudvīpa full of gold, silver, lapis lazuli, mother-of-pearl, agate, coral, amber, and various wonderful treasures, as well as elephants, horses, carriages, and palaces and pavilions made of the seven treasures.

This great donor, having practiced giving in this manner for a full eighty years, has this thought: ‘I have already bestowed upon wandering beings articles of entertainment according to their desires; yet these wandering beings are all now old and decrepit, over eighty years of age, with white hair and wrinkled faces, approaching death before long. I should instruct and guide them through the Buddha-Dharma.’

Immediately he gathers these wandering beings, proclaims the Dharma transformation, instructing, benefiting, and delighting them, so that all at once they attain the srotāpanna path, sakṛdāgāmin path, anāgāmin path, and arhat path, exhausting all outpourings, attaining mastery in deep dhyāna meditations, endowed with the eight liberations.

What do you think: is the merit attained by this great donor plentiful or not?”

Maitreya addressed the Buddha: “World-Honored One! This person’s merit is exceedingly plentiful, immeasurable and boundless. Even if this donor had merely given all articles of joy to wandering beings, his merit would be immeasurable; how much more so causing them to attain the arhat fruit!”

The Buddha addressed Maitreya: “I now clearly tell you: this person, with all articles of joy, gives to wandering beings of the six destinies in four hundred million billion incalculable worlds, and causes them to attain the arhat fruit; yet the merit he attains does not equal that of this fiftieth person who, hearing a single gāthā of the Lotus Sūtra, rejoices in accord: it does not equal one part in a hundred, one part in a thousand, or one part in hundreds of thousands of millions of billions; even calculation and parables cannot know it.

Ajita! If the merit of joyful acceptance of the fiftieth person who successively hears the Lotus Sūtra is immeasurable, boundless, and incalculable like this, how much more so one who initially hears it in the assembly and rejoices in accord! That merit is supreme, immeasurable, boundless, and incalculable, beyond all comparison.

Furthermore, Ajita! If a person, for the sake of this scripture, goes to a monastery, and whether sitting or standing listens and receives it even for an instant, through the causal condition of this merit, in their next rebirth they will attain fine, superior elephants, horses, carriages, palanquins adorned with rare jewels, and ride in heavenly palaces.

If someone sits in the place where the Dharma is expounded, and when another person arrives, urges them to sit and listen, or shares their seat to let them sit, through the causal condition of this merit, in their next rebirth they will attain the seat of Śakra, the seat of Brahmā King, or the seat of a wheel-turning sage king.

Ajita! If someone says to another person: ‘There is a scripture named the Lotus; let us go together to listen to it,’ and that person follows the instruction and listens even for an instant, through the causal condition of this merit, in their next rebirth they will be born together with dhāraṇī Bodhisattvas.

With sharp faculties and wisdom, for hundreds of thousands of lifetimes they will never be mute; their breath will not be foul; their tongue will constantly be free of disease, and their mouth free of disease; their teeth will not be stained, black, yellow, or sparse, nor missing, uneven, or crooked; their lips will not droop, shrink, be rough, ulcerated, cleft, skewed, thick, large, or dark, having no repulsive marks.

Their nose will not be flat or crooked; their facial complexion will not be dark, narrow, long, hollow, or twisted, free of all unpleasing marks. Their lips, tongue, and teeth will all be adorned and fine; their nose long, high, and straight; their face full and round; their eyebrows high and long; their forehead broad and flat; fully endowed with human marks, life after life seeing the Buddha, hearing the Dharma, and faithfully receiving instruction.

Ajita! Look upon this: the merit of encouraging one person to go listen to the Dharma is like this; how much more so single-mindedly listening, speaking, reading, reciting, distinguishing it for others amidst the great assembly, and practicing as taught!”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If a person in the Dharma assembly,

Attains hearing this scripture,

Even down to a single gāthā,

Rejoices in accord and speaks it to others,

In this manner successively teaching,

Reaching the fiftieth person;

The merit attained by this final person,

I will now distinguish it.

Suppose a great donor,

Supplies immeasurable multitudes,

Fulfilling eighty years,

Accordant with all their desires.

Seeing their marks of old age,

White hair and wrinkled faces,

Sparse teeth and withered forms,

Thinking of their approaching death:

“I should now teach them,

Causing them to attain the fruits of the path.”

Immediately through expedient means,

Expounding the genuine Dharma of nirvāṇa:

“The world is entirely unsolid,

Like foam, bubbles, or flames on water;

You all should

Quickly give rise to aversion.”

All the people, hearing this Dharma,

All attain arhatship,

Endowed with the six spiritual superknowledges,

The three knowledges, and eight liberations.

The final fiftieth person,

Hearing a single gāthā and rejoicing in accord,

This person’s merit surpasses the former,

Beyond all parables.

Successively hearing it like this,

That merit is already immeasurable;

How much more so in the Dharma assembly,

One who initially hears and rejoices in accord!

If someone encourages a single person,

Leading them to listen to the Lotus,

Saying: “This scripture is profound and subtle,

Encountered once in tens of millions of kalpas”;

Receiving the teaching, they go to listen,

Even if hearing for an instant;

The karmic retribution of this person,

I will now distinguish and speak of it.

Life after life free of mouth afflictions,

Teeth not sparse, yellow, or black;

Lips neither thick, shrunken, nor cleft,

Free of all repulsive marks;

Tongue not dry, black, or short;

Nose high, long, and straight;

Forehead broad and flat;

Face and eyes upright and dignified;

Delighted in by people who see them;

Breath free of foul stench,

The fragrance of the utpala flower

Constantly issuing from their mouth.

If someone goes intentionally to a monastery,

Wishing to listen to the Lotus Sūtra,

Hearing it for an instant with joy,

I will now speak of their merit:

Reborn among devas and humans,

Attaining wondrous elephants, horses, carriages,

Palanquins of rare jewels,

And riding in heavenly palaces.

If in the place of expounding the Dharma,

One urges another to sit and listen to the scripture,

Through the causal conditions of this merit,

One attains the seats of Śakra, Brahmā, and wheel-turning kings.

How much more so single-mindedly listening,

Explaining its meaning,

And practicing as taught;

That merit is beyond measure!

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XIX

Os méritos do mestre do Dharma

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, the Buddha addressed Nityodyukta Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “If a good man or good woman receives and upholds this Lotus Sūtra, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, that person will attain eight hundred eye merits, twelve hundred ear merits, eight hundred nose merits, twelve hundred tongue merits, eight hundred body merits, and twelve hundred mind merits. With these merits, they adorn the six sense faculties, causing them all to become pure.

This good person, using the pure, natural eyes they were born with, sees all mountains, forests, rivers, and oceans within and without the great three-thousand-fold worlds, downward to the Avīci Hell and upward to the Peak of Existence; and sees all wandering beings within them, as well as their karmic causes and conditions, and the rebirth places of their karmic retributions, completely seeing and knowing them all.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If amidst the great assembly,

With a fearless mind,

One expounds this Lotus Sūtra,

Listen to their merits!

This person attains eight hundred

Extraordinary eye merits;

Adorned through these,

Their eyes are exceedingly pure.

With the eyes given by their parents,

They see the three-thousand-fold worlds,

Inner and outer Mount Merus,

Sumeru and the Iron Ring Mountains,

As well as other mountains and forests,

Waters of great oceans, rivers, and streams;

Downward to the Avīci Hell,

Upward to the Peak of Existence;

All wandering beings within them,

All are fully seen.

Although not having attained the divine eye,

The power of their flesh eye is like this.

“Furthermore, Nityodyukta! If a good man or good woman receives and upholds this scripture, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, they attain twelve hundred ear merits.

With this pure ear, they hear the various languages and sounds within and without the great three-thousand-fold worlds, downward to the Avīci Hell and upward to the Peak of Existence: elephant sounds, horse sounds, ox sounds, carriage sounds, weeping sounds, lamenting sounds, conch sounds, drum sounds, bell sounds, chime sounds, laughing sounds, speaking sounds, men’s sounds, women’s sounds, boys’ sounds, girls’ sounds, Dharma sounds, non-Dharma sounds, sounds of suffering, sounds of joy, common mortals’ sounds, sages’ sounds, joyful sounds, unpleasant sounds;

Sounds of devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, fire sounds, water sounds, wind sounds, hell sounds, animal sounds, hungry ghost sounds, bhikṣus’ sounds, bhikṣuṇīs’ sounds, Śrāvakas’ sounds, Pratyekabuddhas’ sounds, Bodhisattvas’ sounds, and Buddhas’ sounds.

In essence: all sounds within and without the great three-thousand-fold worlds, although they have not yet attained the divine ear, with the pure, ordinary ear given by their parents, they hear and know them all, distinguishing various sounds like this without destroying the ear faculty.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

With the ears given by their parents,

Pure and free of turbidity,

With this ordinary ear they hear

The sounds of the three-thousand-fold worlds:

Sounds of elephants, horses, carriages, and oxen,

Bells, chimes, conches, and drums,

Lutes, harps, pipes, and flutes,

Pure and fine songs;

Listening without attachment,

Countless kinds of human speech,

Hearing, they can understand it all.

Also hearing the sounds of devas,

Subtle and sublime songs,

Hearing the sounds of men and women,

And the voices of boys and girls.

In mountains, rivers, and perilous ravines,

Sounds of kalaviṅka birds,

Jīvaṃjīvaka birds and others,

Hearing all their voices.

The various agonizing torments of hells,

Sounds of sharp suffering,

Hungry ghosts pressed by hunger and thirst,

Sounds of seeking food and drink;

Asuras,

Dwelling by the shores of the great ocean,

When speaking among themselves,

Uttering loud voices.

The expounder of Dharma,

Abiding peacefully here,

Hears all these sounds from afar,

Without damaging the ear faculty.

In worlds of the ten directions,

Birds and beasts calling to one another,

This expounder of Dharma,

Hears them all from here.

In the Brahmā heavens above,

Light-Sound and Universal Purity,

Upward to the Peak of Existence,

The sounds of their speech,

The Dharma teacher dwelling here,

Is completely able to hear.

All assemblies of bhikṣus,

And bhikṣuṇīs,

Whether reading and reciting scriptures,

Or speaking them for others,

The Dharma teacher dwelling here,

Is completely able to hear.

Furthermore, Bodhisattvas,

Reading and reciting the scriptural Dharma,

Or speaking it for others,

Compiling and explaining its meaning,

All such sounds as these,

He is completely able to hear.

The Buddhas, Great Holy Honored Ones,

Who teach and transform wandering beings,

Amidst great assemblies,

Expounding the subtle, wondrous Dharma;

One who upholds this Lotus,

Is completely able to hear.

All sounds within and without

The great three-thousand-fold worlds,

Downward to the Avīci Hell,

Upward to the Peak of Existence,

Hearing all their sounds,

Without damaging the ear faculty;

Because the ear is sharp,

Able to distinguish and know them all.

One who upholds this Lotus,

Though not yet having attained the divine ear,

Merely using the ear given through birth,

Their merits are already like this.

“Furthermore, Nityodyukta! If a good man or good woman receives and upholds this scripture, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, they accomplish eight hundred nose merits.

With this pure nose faculty, they smell various fragrances above, below, within, and without the great three-thousand-fold worlds: sumanā flower fragrance, jātī flower fragrance, mallikā flower fragrance, campaka flower fragrance, pāṭala flower fragrance, red lotus flower fragrance, blue lotus flower fragrance, white lotus flower fragrance, flowering tree fragrance, fruit tree fragrance, sandalwood fragrance, aloeswood fragrance, tamālapatra fragrance, tagara fragrance, and thousands of kinds of blended incenses, whether powdered, pellets, or paste. One who upholds this scripture, dwelling here, can distinguish them all.

Furthermore, they distinguish the fragrances of wandering beings: elephant scent, horse scent, ox and sheep scent, man scent, woman scent, boy scent, girl scent, and the scents of grasses, trees, thickets, and forests—whether near or far, all fragrances are smelled and distinguished without error.

One who upholds this scripture, though dwelling here, also smells the fragrances of heavenly devas: pārijātaka and kovidāra tree fragrances, māndārava flower fragrance, mahā-māndārava flower fragrance, mañjūṣaka flower fragrance, mahā-mañjūṣaka flower fragrance, sandalwood, aloeswood, various powdered incenses, and various flower fragrances—all such blended heavenly fragrances are smelled and known.

They also smell the body scents of devas: the scent of Śakra Devānām Indra enjoying the pleasures of the five desires in his palace; the scent when expounding the Dharma for the Trāyastriṃśa devas in the Hall of Good Dharma; the scent when playing in gardens; and the body scents of other devas, male and female, all smelled from afar. In this manner successively reaching the Brahmā worlds, upward to the body scents of devas of the Peak of Existence, all are smelled, as well as the incenses burned by devas.

And the scent of Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, Bodhisattvas, and the body scents of Buddhas are all smelled from afar, knowing where they dwell. Although smelling these scents, the nose faculty is not impaired or confused; if wishing to distinguish and explain them for others, their memory is without error.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

This person’s nose is pure;

Within this world,

Whether fragrant or foul objects,

They smell and know them all.

Sumanā, jātī,

Tamālapatra, sandalwood,

Aloeswood, and cassia fragrance,

Fragrances of various flowers and fruits,

And knowing the scents of wandering beings,

Scents of men and women;

The expounder of Dharma dwelling far away,

Smells the scent and knows where they are.

Great wheel-turning kings,

Lesser wheel-turning kings and their sons,

Ministers and palace attendants,

Smelling the scent, knows where they are.

Jewels worn upon the body,

Treasures buried in the earth,

And the wheel-turning king’s precious women,

Smelling the scent, knows where they are.

People’s personal ornaments,

Clothing, necklaces,

And various paste incenses,

Smelling the scent, knows their bodies.

Devas walking or sitting,

Sporting and displaying spiritual transformations;

One who upholds this Lotus,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Flowers and fruits of trees,

And the fragrance of ghee,

The upholder of the scripture dwelling here,

Knows where they all are.

In deep, perilous mountain places,

Where sandalwood trees blossom,

Wandering beings dwelling within them,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

The Iron Ring Mountains, the great ocean,

And wandering beings within the earth,

The upholder of the scripture smelling the scent,

Knows where they all are.

Asura men and women,

And their retinues,

When fighting or sporting,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

In wildernesses and narrow passes,

Lions, elephants, tigers, wolves,

Wild oxen, and water buffaloes,

Smelling the scent, knows where they are.

If there are pregnant women,

Whose child’s sex is not yet known,

Deformed or non-human,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Through the power of smelling scent,

Knowing the initial pregnancy,

Whether it will be brought to term or not,

And if she will safely bear a blessed child.

Through the power of smelling scent,

Knowing the thoughts of men and women,

Minds of lust, delusion, or anger,

And knowing those who cultivate goodness.

Hidden treasuries within the earth,

Gold, silver, and precious gems,

Stored within bronze vessels,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Various necklaces,

Whose value none can know;

Smelling the scent, knows their value,

Their origin, and location.

All flowers in the heavens,

Māndārava, mañjūṣaka,

And the pārijātaka tree,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

All palaces in the heavens,

Superior, middling, and inferior,

Adorned with various jewel flowers,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Heavenly gardens and excellent palaces,

Pavilions and the Hall of Good Dharma,

Sporting within them,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

When devas listen to the Dharma,

Or indulge in the five desires,

Coming, going, walking, sitting, or lying down,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Robes worn by heavenly maidens,

Adorned with fine flower scents,

When whirling and sporting,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Successively ascending in this manner,

Reaching the Brahmā worlds,

Those entering or emerging from dhyāna,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Light-Sound and Universal Purity heavens,

Upward to the Peak of Existence,

Newly born or passing away,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Assemblies of bhikṣus,

Constantly vigorous in the Dharma,

Whether sitting or walking,

Reading and reciting scriptural Dharma,

Or beneath forest trees,

Concentrated in seated meditation;

The upholder of the scripture smelling the scent,

Knows where they all are.

Bodhisattvas firm in resolve,

Sitting in meditation or reading and reciting,

Or speaking the Dharma for others,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

The World-Honored Ones in every direction,

Revered by all,

Pitying beings and expounding the Dharma,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Wandering beings before the Buddha,

Rejoicing upon hearing the scripture,

Practicing according to the Dharma,

Smelling the scent, can know them all.

Although not yet having attained the Bodhisattva’s

Nose born of uncontaminated Dharma,

This upholder of the scripture,

First attains such marks of the nose as these.

“Furthermore, Nityodyukta! If a good man or good woman receives and upholds this scripture, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, they attain twelve hundred tongue merits.

Whether fine or repulsive, delicious or unpalatable, and all bitter and astringent substances, when placed upon their tongue faculty, all transform into superior flavors, like heavenly amṛta, none that are not delicious.

If with the tongue faculty amidst the great assembly they proclaim anything, uttering a deep, wondrous sound, it can enter their hearts, causing them all to attain joy and delight.

Furthermore, sons of devas, heavenly maidens, Śakra, Brahmā, and devas, hearing this deep, wondrous sound and the orderly sequence of speech expounded, will all come to listen. And Nagas, Naga maidens, yakṣas, yakṣa maidens, gandharvas, gandharva maidens, asuras, asura maidens, garuḍas, garuḍa maidens, kiṃnaras, kiṃnara maidens, mahoragas, and mahoraga maidens, for the sake of hearing the Dharma, will all come to draw close, revering and making offerings.

And bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, kings, princes, ministers, retinues, lesser wheel-turning kings, great wheel-turning kings, with their seven treasures, thousand sons, and inner and outer retinues, riding their palaces, will come together to listen to the Dharma, because this Bodhisattva skillfully expounds the Dharma. Brāhmaṇas, householders, and the people of the land will, for the rest of their lives, attend upon and make offerings to him.

Furthermore, Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, Bodhisattvas, and Buddhas constantly delight in seeing him. Wherever this person resides, the Buddhas all face that direction to expound the Dharma; he is able fully to receive and uphold all Buddha-dharmas, and can also emit deep, wondrous Dharma sounds.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

This person’s tongue faculty is pure,

Never receiving bad tastes;

Whatever is eaten,

All turns into amṛta.

With a deep, pure, wondrous sound,

Expounding the Dharma amidst the great assembly,

With causal conditions and parables,

Guiding the minds of wandering beings;

Listeners all rejoice,

Setting forth superior offerings.

Devas, Nagas, yakṣas,

And asuras,

All with reverent minds,

Come together to listen to the Dharma;

This expounder of Dharma,

If wishing with a wondrous sound

To fill the three-thousand-fold worlds,

Can reach it as desired.

Great and lesser wheel-turning kings,

With their thousand sons and retinues,

With palms joined and reverent minds,

Constantly come to listen and receive the Dharma.

Devas, Nagas, yakṣas,

Rākṣasas, and piśācas,

Also with joyful minds,

Constantly delight in coming to make offerings.

Brahmā Kings, Mara Kings,

Īśvara, and Maheśvara,

Such assemblies of devas,

Constantly come to where he is.

The Buddhas and their disciples,

Hearing his sound of expounding the Dharma,

Constantly remember and guard him,

And at times manifest their bodies for him.

“Furthermore, Nityodyukta! If a good man or good woman receives and upholds this scripture, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, they attain eight hundred body merits.

They attain a pure body, like pure lapis lazuli, which wandering beings delight in seeing. Because of the purity of their body, wandering beings of the great three-thousand-fold worlds—at the time of birth, at the time of death, high or low, fair or ugly, born in good or evil destinies—all appear within it. And the Iron Ring Mountains, Great Iron Ring Mountains, Mount Meru, Mahāmeru, and other mountains, as well as the wandering beings within them, all appear within it. Downward to the Avīci Hell and upward to the Peak of Existence, all things and wandering beings appear within it. If Śrāvakas, Pratyekabuddhas, Bodhisattvas, or Buddhas expound the Dharma, all manifest their forms and images within his body.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

If one upholds the Lotus,

Their body is exceedingly pure,

Like pure lapis lazuli,

Delighted in by all wandering beings.

Like a pure, bright mirror,

Fully reflecting all forms and images;

The Bodhisattva within his pure body,

Sees all that exists in the world;

He alone clearly understands,

While other people cannot see.

Within the three-thousand-fold worlds,

All living creatures,

Devas, humans, asuras,

Hells, ghosts, and animals,

All such forms and images,

All manifest within his body.

The palaces of devas,

Upward to the Peak of Existence,

The Iron Ring and Mount Meru,

And Mahāmeru,

Waters of great oceans,

All manifest within his body.

The Buddhas and Śrāvakas,

Buddha-children, Bodhisattvas,

Whether solitary or amidst assemblies,

Expounding the Dharma, all manifest.

Although not having attained the uncontaminated,

Wondrous body of Dharma-nature,

Within his pure, ordinary body,

All things manifest within it.

“Furthermore, Nityodyukta! If a good man or good woman, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, receives and upholds this scripture, whether reading, reciting, explaining, or copying it, they attain twelve hundred mind merits.

With this pure mind faculty, hearing even a single gāthā or a single phrase, they penetrate immeasurable, boundless meanings. Having understood this meaning, they can expound a single phrase or a single gāthā for one month, four months, or up to a year; all the dharmas expounded, accordant with their meaning, will not conflict with the genuine mark.

If speaking of worldly books, governing speech, or livelihoods, all accord with the correct Dharma. Wandering beings of the six destinies in the great three-thousand-fold worlds, the movements of their minds, the actions of their minds, and the discursive play of their minds, they know them all.

Although they have not yet attained uncontaminated wisdom, their mind faculty is pure like this. Whatever this person contemplates, ponders, or speaks, all is the Buddha-Dharma, without falsehood, and was also spoken in the scriptures of past Buddhas.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

This person’s mind is pure,

Bright, sharp, and free of defilement;

With this wondrous mind faculty,

Knowing superior, middling, and inferior dharmas;

Hearing even a single gāthā,

Penetrating immeasurable meanings;

Successively expounding according to the Dharma,

For a month, four months, up to a year.

Within and without this world,

All wandering beings,

Devas, Nagas, humans,

Yakṣas, ghosts, and spirits,

Those in the six destinies,

Whatever they contemplate,

As the reward for upholding the Lotus,

All at once he fully knows.

Countless Buddhas of the ten directions,

Adorned with marks of a hundred merits,

Expounding the Dharma for wandering beings,

He hears and can uphold it all.

Contemplating immeasurable meanings,

Expounding the Dharma immeasurably,

From beginning to end never forgetting or erring,

Because of upholding the Lotus.

Fully knowing the marks of all dharmas,

Recognizing sequences according to the meaning,

Penetrating names and language,

Expounding as he knows.

Whatever this person speaks,

Is all the Dharma of past Buddhas;

Because of expounding this Dharma,

Dwelling amidst the assembly without fear.

One who upholds the Lotus Sūtra,

Has a mind faculty pure like this;

Though not yet having attained the uncontaminated,

First possesses such marks as these.

This person upholding this scripture,

Abides peacefully on a rare ground;

By all wandering beings,

Joyfully loved and revered.

Able with thousands of kinds

Of skillful words,

Distinguishing and expounding the Dharma,

Because of upholding the Lotus Sūtra.

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XX

O bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, the Buddha addressed Mahāsthāmaprāpta Bodhisattva Mahāsattva: “You should now know! If bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, or upāsikās who uphold the Lotus Sūtra are reviled, abused, or slandered by anyone with an evil mouth, they incur great sinful retribution, as spoken before; the merits attained by the upholders are as spoken just now: the purity of eyes, ears, nose, tongue, body, and mind.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! In the distant past, immeasurable, boundless, inconceivable incalculable kalpas ago, there was a Buddha named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. His kalpa was named Vinirbhoga, and his land was named Mahāsaṃbhava.

That Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha, in that world, expounded the Dharma for devas, humans, and asuras: for those seeking to be Śrāvakas, expounding the Dharma responding to the Four Truths, to cross beyond birth, old age, sickness, and death, to ultimate nirvāṇa; for those seeking to be Pratyekabuddhas, expounding the Dharma responding to the Twelve Links of Dependent Origination; for Bodhisattvas, on account of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, expounding the Dharma responding to the Six Pāramitās, to ultimate Buddha wisdom.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! The lifespan of this Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha was forty thousand billion inconceivable numbers of Ganges sands of kalpas; the kalpas of the Correct Dharma abiding in the world were equal to the dust motes of a single Jambudvīpa; the kalpas of the Semblance Dharma abiding were equal to the dust motes of the four continents. That Buddha, having benefited wandering beings, passed into extinction.

After the Correct Dharma and Semblance Dharma were entirely exhausted, in that land another Buddha appeared, also named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān. In this manner successively, there were twenty thousand billion Buddhas, all with the same name.

After the initial Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Tathāgata passed into extinction, and after the Correct Dharma ended, during the Semblance Dharma, bhikṣus of arrogant pride possessed great power and influence. At that time, there was a Bodhisattva bhikṣu named Sadāparibhūta.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! For what causal condition was he named Sadāparibhūta? Whenever this bhikṣu saw anyone—whether bhikṣu, bhikṣuṇī, upāsaka, or upāsikā—he bowed down to them in homage, praised them, and spoke these words: ‘I deeply revere you all; I dare not slight or despise you. For what reason? You all practice the Bodhisattva path and will become Buddhas.’

And this bhikṣu did not specifically read or recite the scriptures, but merely practiced bowing in homage; even seeing the fourfold assembly from afar, he would intentionally go to bow in homage, praise them, and speak these words: ‘I dare not slight you; you will all become Buddhas.’

Among the fourfold assembly, there were those who gave rise to anger and impure minds, reviling him with evil mouths, saying: ‘Where does this ignorant bhikṣu come from? Claiming of himself: “I do not slight you,” and conferring prophecies upon us that we will become Buddhas! We have no use for such false prophecies.’

Passing through many years in this manner, constantly reviled, he gave rise to no anger, constantly speaking: ‘You will become Buddhas.’ When he spoke these words, people sometimes struck him with sticks, wood, tiles, or stones; fleeing and standing far away, he still proclaimed in a loud voice: ‘I dare not slight you; you will all become Buddhas!’

Because he constantly spoke these words, the bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās of arrogant pride named him Sadāparibhūta.

When this bhikṣu approached death, in open space he heard the twenty thousand billion verses of the Lotus Sūtra previously expounded by Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja Buddha, fully able to receive and uphold them all, immediately attaining the purity of the eye faculty, and the purity of ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind faculties as spoken above. Having attained the purity of these six faculties, his lifespan was lengthened by two hundred million billion inconceivable years, and he expounded this Lotus Sūtra widely for people.

At that time, the fourfold assembly of arrogant pride—bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās—who had despised this person and given him the name Sadāparibhūta, seeing that he had attained great spiritual powers, the power of joyful and unhindered eloquence, and the power of great wholesome tranquility, hearing what he expounded, all believed, submitted, and followed him.

This Bodhisattva further transformed thousands of billions of multitudes, causing them to abide in anuttarasamyaksambodhi. After his life ended, he encountered two thousand billion Buddhas, all named Candrasūryapradīpa; within their Dharma, he expounded this Lotus Sūtra.

By this causal condition, he further encountered two thousand billion Buddhas, all named Meghasvaradīparāja; within the Dharma of these Buddhas, receiving, upholding, reading, reciting, and expounding this scripture for the fourfold assembly, he attained this purity of the ordinary eye, and the purity of ear, nose, tongue, body, and mind faculties, expounding the Dharma amidst the fourfold assembly with mind free of fear.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! This Bodhisattva Mahāsattva Sadāparibhūta made offerings to such numerous Buddhas as these, revering, honoring, praising them, and planting roots of virtue; afterward, he further encountered thousands of billions of Buddhas, also expounding this scripture within the Dharma of those Buddhas, his merits accomplished, destined to become a Buddha.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! What do you think: could the Bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta at that time have been someone else? It was I myself. If in past lives I had not received, upheld, read, recited, and expounded this scripture for others, I would not have been able swiftly to attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi. Because under past Buddhas I received, upheld, read, recited, and expounded this scripture for others, I swiftly attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! At that time, the fourfold assembly—bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās—because with angry minds they despised and scorned me, for two hundred billion kalpas never encountered a Buddha, never heard the Dharma, and never saw the Saṅgha; for a thousand kalpas in the Avīci Hell they underwent great suffering and affliction. Having exhausted this transgression, they again encountered Bodhisattva Sadāparibhūta, who taught and transformed them in anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

Mahāsthāmaprāpta, what do you think: could those in the fourfold assembly at that time who constantly despised this Bodhisattva have been someone else? They are the five hundred Bodhisattvas in this assembly, such as Bhadrapāla; the five hundred bhikṣus, such as Siṃhacandra; and the five hundred upāsakas, such as Sugatacetanā, who are all irreversible from anuttarasamyaksambodhi!

Mahāsthāmaprāpta! You should know that this Lotus Sūtra greatly benefits all Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, able to cause them to arrive at anuttarasamyaksambodhi. Therefore, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, should constantly receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, and copy this scripture.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

In the past there was a Buddha,

Named Bhīṣmagarjitasvararāja,

Of immeasurable spiritual wisdom,

Guiding all beings;

Devas, humans, Nagas, and spirits,

Together made offerings to him.

After this Buddha passed into extinction,

When the Dharma was about to end,

There was a Bodhisattva,

Named Sadāparibhūta.

At that time the fourfold assembly,

Were attached to dharmas.

Bodhisattva Paribhūta,

Went to where they were,

And addressed them:

“I do not slight you;

You practice the path,

And will all become Buddhas.”

All the people, hearing this,

Slighted, defamed, and reviled him;

Bodhisattva Paribhūta,

Was able to endure it.

When his transgressions were exhausted,

Approaching the end of his life,

He attained hearing this scripture,

And his six faculties were purified.

Through spiritual powers,

His lifespan was lengthened,

And again for all people,

He widely expounded this scripture.

The multitudes attached to dharmas,

All received the Bodhisattva’s

Teaching, transformation, and accomplishment,

Causing them to abide in the Buddha path.

When Paribhūta’s life ended,

He encountered countless Buddhas;

Because of expounding this scripture,

He attained immeasurable merit;

Gradually fulfilling virtues,

He swiftly accomplished the Buddha path.

The Paribhūta of that time,

Was none other than myself.

The fourfold assembly at that time,

Who were attached to dharmas,

Hearing Paribhūta say:

“You will become Buddhas”;

By this causal condition,

Encountered countless Buddhas.

The assembly of five hundred Bodhisattvas

In this gathering,

Along with the fourfold assembly,

Pure faithful men and women,

Now standing before me,

Listening to the Dharma, are them.

I in past lives,

Urged these people,

To hear and receive this scripture,

The foremost Dharma;

Opening and teaching people,

Causing them to abide in nirvāṇa;

Life after life receiving and upholding

Such a scripture as this.

Throughout tens of billions of kalpas,

Down to the inconceivable,

Only then at certain times does one hear

This Lotus Sūtra.

Throughout tens of billions of kalpas,

Down to the inconceivable,

The Buddhas, World-Honored Ones,

Only at certain times expound this scripture.

Therefore practitioners,

After the Buddha’s extinction,

Hearing such a scripture as this,

Should not give rise to doubt;

They should single-mindedly

Widely expound this scripture,

Life after life encountering Buddhas,

Swiftly accomplishing the Buddha path.

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XXI

Os poderes espirituais do Tathāgata

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas equal to the dust motes of a thousand worlds who had welled forth from the earth, all before the Buddha single-mindedly joined their palms, gazed upon the venerable countenance, and addressed the Buddha:

“World-Honored One! After the Buddha’s extinction, in the lands where the World-Honored One’s emanation bodies dwell and in the places where they pass into extinction, we will widely expound this scripture. For what reason? We ourselves wish to attain this genuine, pure, great Dharma, to receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, and make offerings to it.”

Then the World-Honored One, before Mañjuśrī and immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas who had long dwelled in the Sahā world, as well as bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans—before the entire assembly, manifested great spiritual powers: extending his broad and long tongue upward to the Brahmā Heaven, and from all pores emitted light of immeasurable, countless colors, universally illuminating worlds of the ten directions.

The Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath the jewel trees also did likewise, extending broad and long tongues and emitting immeasurable light.

When Śākyamuni Buddha and the Buddhas beneath the jewel trees manifested their spiritual powers, a full hundred thousand years elapsed, after which they drew back their tongues. Simultaneously they cleared their throats and snapped their fingers together; these two sounds pervaded worlds of the Buddhas in the ten directions, and the earth shook in six ways.

The wandering beings within them—devas, Nagas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, and non-humans—through the Buddha’s spiritual power, all saw the Buddhas seated upon lion seats beneath immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of jewel trees in this Sahā world; and saw Śākyamuni Buddha together with Prabhūtaratna Tathāgata seated upon the lion seat within the jewel stūpa; and saw immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas and the fourfold assembly reverently surrounding Śākyamuni Buddha.

Having seen this, they all rejoiced greatly, attaining what they had never had before.

Immediately the devas in open space proclaimed in loud voices: “Across these immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable worlds, there is a land named Sahā; within it is a Buddha named Śākyamuni, who now for the sake of Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas expounds the Great Vehicle sūtra named the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas. You should rejoice in accord with a deep mind, and should bow down and make offerings to Śākyamuni Buddha!”

All those wandering beings, hearing the voices in open space, joined their palms toward the Sahā world and said: “Homage to Śākyamuni Buddha! Homage to Śākyamuni Buddha!”

With various flowers, incense, necklaces, banners, canopies, personal ornaments, and rare treasures, they all scattered them together toward the Sahā world from afar. The scattered objects, arriving from the ten directions like gathering clouds, transformed into a jewel canopy, covering all the Buddhas here above. At that time, worlds of the ten directions penetrated without hindrance, like a single Buddha land.

Then the Buddha addressed Viśiṣṭacāritra and the assembly of Bodhisattvas: “The spiritual powers of the Buddhas are immeasurable, boundless, and inconceivable like this. If I, through these spiritual powers, throughout immeasurable, boundless hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, for the sake of entrustment, were to speak of the merits of this scripture, I could not exhaust them.

In essence: all dharmas possessed by the Tathāgata, all sovereign spiritual powers of the Tathāgata, all secret essential treasuries of the Tathāgata, and all profound matters of the Tathāgata are proclaimed and revealed in this scripture.

Therefore, you all, after the Tathāgata’s extinction, should single-mindedly receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, and practice it as taught. In whatever land, if there are those who receive, uphold, read, recite, explain, copy, or practice it as taught, or wherever copies of the scripture reside—whether in a garden, in a forest, beneath a tree, in a monastery, in a white-robed layman’s house, in a palace hall, or in a mountain valley or wilderness—in all these places one should erect a stūpa and make offerings.

For what reason? You should know that this place is the place of awakening: here the Buddhas attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi, here the Buddhas turned the wheel of Dharma, and here the Buddhas entered parinirvāṇa.”

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

The Buddhas, saviors of the world,

Abiding in great spiritual powers,

To delight wandering beings,

Manifest immeasurable spiritual powers;

Their tongues reach the Brahmā Heaven,

Their bodies emit countless lights;

For those who seek the Buddha path,

They manifest these rare matters.

The sound of the Buddhas clearing their throats,

And the sound of snapping their fingers,

Universally heard in lands of the ten directions,

The earth shaking in six ways.

Because after the Buddha’s extinction,

One can uphold this scripture,

All the Buddhas rejoice,

Manifesting immeasurable spiritual powers.

To entrust this scripture,

Praising those who receive and uphold it,

Throughout immeasurable kalpas,

Still it cannot be exhausted.

The merits of this person,

Are boundless and without end,

Like open space in the ten directions,

Whose limits cannot be found.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Have already seen me,

And have seen Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

And all the emanation Buddhas;

They also see me today,

Teaching and transforming Bodhisattvas.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Cause me and my emanation bodies,

And the extinct Prabhūtaratna Buddha,

To completely rejoice.

Present Buddhas of the ten directions,

As well as past and future Buddhas,

They also behold and make offerings to,

And cause to attain joy.

What the Buddhas at the place of awakening

Attained as the secret essential Dharma,

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Before long will also attain.

Those who can uphold this scripture,

Regarding the meanings of all dharmas,

Their names and expressions,

Delight in speaking without end,

Like wind in open space,

Completely free of hindrances.

After the Tathāgata’s extinction,

Knowing the scriptures spoken by the Buddha,

Their causal conditions and sequences,

Expounding according to the meaning as it is;

Like the light of the sun and moon,

Able to dispel all darkness.

This person traveling through the world,

Can extinguish the darkness of wandering beings,

Teaching immeasurable Bodhisattvas,

Ultimately to abide in the One Vehicle.

Therefore those who possess wisdom,

Hearing this benefit of merit,

After my extinction,

Should receive and uphold this scripture;

This person regarding the Buddha path,

Definitive and without doubt!

← Capítulo anteriorPróximo capítulo →

Capítulo XXII

A transmissão

Este capítulo ainda está em tradução para o português; é exibido provisoriamente em inglês.

At that time, Śākyamuni Buddha arose from the Dharma seat, manifested great spiritual powers, and with his right hand rubbed the crowns of immeasurable Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, speaking these words:

“Throughout immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, I cultivated this difficult-to-attain Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; I now entrust it to you all. You should single-mindedly propagate this Dharma, widely causing it to increase.”

In this manner three times he rubbed the crowns of the Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, speaking these words: “Throughout immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable kalpas, I cultivated this difficult-to-attain Dharma of anuttarasamyaksambodhi; I now entrust it to you all. You should receive, uphold, read, recite, and widely proclaim this Dharma, causing all wandering beings universally to hear and know it.

For what reason? The Tathāgata possesses great love and compassion, free of stinginess, and free of fear; he is able to give to wandering beings the Buddha’s wisdom, the Tathāgata’s wisdom, and spontaneous wisdom; the Tathāgata is the great donor to all wandering beings. You too should follow and learn the Tathāgata’s Dharma, not giving rise to stinginess.

In future ages, if there is a good man or good woman who believes in the Tathāgata’s wisdom, you should expound this Lotus Sūtra for them, causing them to hear and know it, in order to cause that person to attain the Buddha’s wisdom. If there are wandering beings who do not believe and accept it, you should instruct, benefit, and delight them within other deep dharmas of the Tathāgata. If you can act like this, you will have repaid the kindness of all Buddhas.”
